{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01PBPF3CAn2mSh86TMMqCnL8","type":"tool_result","content":"# Deliver a user's conversation list over the WebSocket protocol\n\nIssue: re-cinq/HALEngine#144\nLabels: type:feature, size:M, area:api, area:compliance, epic:extension-points\n\nPart of #35\n\n## Why\n\n#143 gives a consumer `store.listFor(userId)`.\nIt does not give a **client** anything: the browser holding the socket cannot reach the store, so\nevery consumer that wants a history sidebar has to stand up its own authenticated HTTP route over\nthe same data the engine already authenticated on the upgrade. That is a second authorisation\npath to the same personal data, written once per consumer, each one able to get the ownership\ncheck wrong in its own way — the exact failure #45 went out of its way to avoid for resume.\n\nA consumer may have no such route to extend: an engine deployment behind an api with a session\nstore and no conversation endpoints of its own has nothing to add this to. The engine already knows who is connected and already\nreplays a conversation on resume; answering \"which conversations do I have\" over the same socket\nis the smaller surface.\n\n## What to do\n\n- `src/types/messages.ts`, additive only:\n  - `ListConversationsMessage {type: 'list_conversations'; limit?: number; before?: string}` into\n    `IncomingMessage`. There is no user id field, and that is the point: the list is always the\n    connection's authenticated user's.\n  - `ConversationListMessage {type: 'conversation_list'; conversations: ConversationSummary[]}`\n    into `OutgoingMessage`, where `ConversationSummary` is `SessionSummary` with its two times as\n    ISO strings — `Date` does not survive `JSON.stringify` as a `Date`, and the protocol types\n    describe what goes on the wire.\n- `validation.ts`: a `list_conversations` case — `limit`, when present, an integer 1–200;\n  `before`, when present, a string `Date.parse` accepts. Anything else is `INVALID_MESSAGE`, as\n  the two existing cases do it.\n- Handling: `createMessageHandler` takes the session store and the new options alongside the\n  orchestrator (`createServer` already holds both), and answers `list_conversations` on the\n  non-locked path beside `ping` — a history request is not a turn and must not queue behind a\n  streaming answer.\n- The user id comes from `ws.userId`, never from the frame. Add the regression:\n  a frame carrying an extra `userId` of another user is answered with the connecting user's own\n  list.\n- Opt-in: `HalEngineConfig.transport.history?: {enabled: boolean}`, default off, threaded through\n  `createServer`'s options like `resume`. With it off, `list_conversations` is answered with an\n  `error` frame and no list.\n- A store that implements no `listFor` is answered with an `error`, not an empty list: a client\n  that renders \"no conversations\" for a user who has several is a worse answer than one that\n  renders a failure. `ErrorCodes` gains one code for both cases (`UNSUPPORTED`), additive.\n- Picking a conversation needs nothing new: the client reconnects with `?sessionId=<id>`, which\n  #45 already ships with the ownership check and the replay. No in-band switch frame, no second\n  replay path.\n- Specs and docs: extend `specs/hal-engine-websocket-protocol/spec.md` (the two frames, their\n  field tables, the authorisation rule) and the conversation-list spec; add § Conversation list\n  to `docs/getting-started.md` by editing `example/full-config.ts` and running `npm run docs:fix`\n  — that listing sits under `<!-- doc-block: example/full-config.ts#full-config -->` and\n  `npm run docs:check` fails a hand-edited block.\n\n## Decisions\n\n- **A frame, not a REST route.** The REST chat routes are demo-only and are not where a consumer is meant to build. The WebSocket is where the caller is\n  already authenticated, once per connection, by the consumer's own `wsAuth`.\n- **Its own opt-in flag, separate from `resume`.** Resume hands a client back a conversation it\n  was already in; a list tells it what else the user has. A deployer who turned on resume did not\n  thereby agree to the second disclosure, and a config that conflates them cannot be audited.\n- **Answered off the per-session lock.** `PerSessionLock` serialises turns (#47). A list request\n  that queued behind a two-minute streaming answer would look broken, and it mutates nothing.\n- **An error, not an empty list, when it cannot be answered.** See above; silence that looks like\n  data is the one failure mode a history view cannot recover from.\n- **Answered when asked, never pushed.** No `conversation_list` arrives unsolicited: a client\n  that just started a conversation knows, and a push would need a fan-out across the sockets a\n  resumed session can be open on.\n- **The frame is accepted by `validateMessage` whether or not the feature is on**, and the\n  handler is what refuses it. Validation is static and has no config; the alternative is\n  threading options into a pure validator to turn a type into \"unknown\".\n\n## Acceptance criteria\n\n- [ ] `src/types/messages.ts` gains only new interfaces and their two union members (**amended:** four interfaces, not two — each frame needs a type for its own payload, `ConversationCursor` for what a client sends back and `ConversationSummary` for a row on the wire) — a\n      `git diff` of that file touches no existing line, and a fixture pins that every message\n      literal valid before this change still compiles\n- [ ] `validateMessage({type: 'list_conversations'})` is valid; with `limit: 0`, `limit: 201`,\n      `limit: 1.5`, `limit: '10'` or `before: 'not a date'` it is invalid with a message naming\n      the field\n- [ ] With `history: {enabled: true}` and a store holding three conversations for the user, a\n      `list_conversations` frame is answered with exactly one `conversation_list` carrying three\n      summaries, newest first, each `createdAt` and `updatedAt` an ISO string, and no\n      `stream_end`\n- [ ] A frame carrying `{type: 'list_conversations', userId: '<another user>'}` is answered with\n      the connecting user's own list, and the store's `listFor` is called with `ws.userId` —\n      asserted on the recorded argument, not only on the frame\n- [ ] `limit` and `before` reach `listFor` as the frame sent them. **Amended:** `before` is a\n      `{updatedAt, sessionId}` cursor of ISO strings, not a `Date`. This was written before\n      #143's review found that paging on a timestamp alone drops a conversation whenever two\n      share an activity time, so the store's cursor became a composite that accepts an ISO\n      string; a `Date` cannot carry the tie-break. The handler passes `{limit, before}` with\n      both possibly `undefined` rather than omitting the argument, which is the same call\n- [ ] With `history` absent, the same frame is answered with one `error` frame whose code is\n      `UNSUPPORTED` and no `conversation_list`\n- [ ] With `history: {enabled: true}` against a store implementing no `listFor`, the answer is\n      the same `error` frame, and `grep` shows no code path that sends `conversations: []`\n- [ ] A `listFor` that rejects is answered with one `error` frame whose code is `SERVER_ERROR`\n      and whose message carries no store detail, the socket stays open, and the failure is\n      logged with the session id and no user content. **Added during the build:** a store throwing\n      the new exported `StoreCannotList` is answered `UNSUPPORTED` instead, so an injected\n      collection without a `find` reads as the misconfiguration it is rather than a database\n      that failed\n- [ ] A `list_conversations` sent while a `user_message` is streaming on the same session is\n      answered before that turn's `stream_end` — it does not wait on the lock\n- [ ] A client that takes an id from a `conversation_list` and reconnects with `?sessionId=<id>`\n      receives `resumed: true` and that conversation's replay, with no new transport code: the\n      test exercises the existing #45 path end to end\n- [ ] `ConnectedMessage` is unchanged by this issue\n- [ ] **GDPR.** The protocol spec states that `conversation_list` carries one authenticated\n      user's own conversation metadata and no conversation content, that the user id is taken\n      from the connection and never from the frame, and that `transport.history` is the\n      deployer's decision to make a user's history reachable from the client; `SessionResumeOptions`'\n      retention note is mirrored on the new options type, pointing at #41\n- [ ] `CHANGELOG.md` carries an entry under `## [Unreleased]` written for somebody installing the\n      package\n- [ ] The `docs/getting-started.md` change is made in `example/full-config.ts` and the doc\n      regenerated with `npm run docs:fix`, not typed into the markdown\n- [ ] Spec statements carry trailing `([validated by: ...](...#Lnn))` citations, `Status` rows\n      match coverage, and `npm run check:spec-links`, `npm run check:spec-status` and\n      `npm run spec:names` pass\n- [ ] The version bump is MINOR — new optional config, two additive protocol frames, one new\n      error code — and the PR body says so; no migration guide is owed\n- [ ] `npm run verify` passes and the coverage floors for the touched groups hold or rise\n\n## Amendments\n\nThree things changed between writing this issue and building it, each marked in the criterion it\naffects:\n\n1. **`before` is a cursor, not a timestamp.** #143's review found that paging on `updatedAt` alone\n   silently drops a conversation when two share an activity time. The store's cursor became\n   `{updatedAt, sessionId}`, so the frame carries both halves and the validator refuses one half\n   alone. Built as originally written, the transport would have reintroduced the defect the store\n   had just been fixed for.\n2. **`messages.ts` gains four interfaces, not two** — the two frames plus the two payload types\n   they describe.\n3. **`StoreCannotList` is new public surface**, beyond what this issue specified: without it a\n   permanent store misconfiguration cannot be told from a transient failure, in either the client\n   frame or the log.\n\nTwo decisions the criteria did not anticipate:\n\n- **`transport.history` without `transport.resume` is useless, and now says so.** `?sessionId=` is\n  read only when resume is enabled, so a list whose rows cannot be opened is a deployment slip:\n  the engine warns at startup and the documentation says so where it tells a client to reconnect\n  with a row's id. Refusing to start was considered and rejected as too blunt for a library.\n- **The conversation the connection is in is listed like any other**, so a fresh connect sees a row\n  of its own holding no entries. Filtering it out was rejected: it is a real conversation the user\n  is in, and it is their newest the moment they send anything.\n\n## Tech notes\n\nDepends on #143: without `listFor` there is\nnothing to send.\n\nKey paths: `src/types/messages.ts`, `src/types/session.ts` (`ErrorCodes`),\n`src/transport/ws/{validation,messageHandler,sender}.ts`, `src/transport/createServer.ts`,\n`src/config.ts`, `example/full-config.ts`, `specs/hal-engine-websocket-protocol/spec.md`.\n\nOut of scope: a preview or title on the summaries (its own issue); deleting a conversation from\nthe client, which would put an erasure primitive on the wire and needs its own authorisation\ndecision; paging state held server-side — the client pages with `before`; an operator view of\nanother user's conversations, which is an authorisation question of its own; and live sync between open tabs,\nwhich #124 ruled out.\n\nRefs #143, #145, #41, #45, #47, #124\n\n\n\n\n---\n## CI reported failures on 6c0a42c9960a0247e33b28157d3f80cae03e89ab\n\nThe build for your last push is red. These checks failed: eslint, test, spec-names, changelog\n\nWhere a failed step is named below, run only that step's command; otherwise map each name to the job that publishes it and run only that job's command.\n\n```\n### eslint (failure)\n\nsrc/transport/conversationList.acceptance.test.ts\n   23:109  error  7 lines (58 tokens) duplicated with src/transport/sessionResume.test.ts:51-58 error  7 lines (58 tokens) duplicated with src/transport/conversationList.acceptance.test.ts:23-29   re-lint/no-duplicate-code\n   63:13  error  \n...(truncated)\n```\n\n## The previous round reported\n\n- Done: Validate a bare list_sessions frame (facet 2, bare-frame part)\n- Next: Validate limit and before on list_sessions in validation.ts\n---\n# Definition of Done\n\n> #143 gives a consumer `store.listFor(userId)` inside the server process. The client on the other end of the WebSocket has no way to ask for it, and the consumer who wants a conversation picker without standing up their own REST endpoint has to write one anyway.\n\n**Strategy: `direct`** — `createServer` and the WebSocket upgrade/message pipeline already exist and accept frames over a real WebSocket connection, so acceptance tests can connect today, send `list_sessions` frames, and fail directly on the missing `session_list` and `list_sessions` validation/dispatch behaviour.\n\n## Done when these pass\n\n- [ ] **listing conversations over the WebSocket protocol › answers list_sessions for the authenticated user with ISO dates, forwarding limit and before while ignoring any client userId** — Sends `{type: 'list_sessions', limit, before, userId}` over a connected WebSocket and verifies `store.listFor` is called with the socket's authenticated `userId` (`session.userId`) and `{limit, before}`, answering `{type: 'session_list', sessions}` with `createdAt` and `updatedAt` serialised as ISO-8601 strings.\n  `src/transport/conversationList.acceptance.test.ts`\n\n- [ ] **listing conversations over the WebSocket protocol › answers an empty session_list when the store has no listFor, and SERVER_ERROR with a ws error log when listFor throws** — Verifies that a store without `listFor` answers `{type: 'session_list', sessions: []}` and a store whose `listFor` throws/rejects logs an error under category `'ws'` with the userId and error message and answers `{type: 'error', code: 'SERVER_ERROR'}`.\n  `src/transport/conversationList.acceptance.test.ts`\n\n- [ ] **listing conversations over the WebSocket protocol › accepts a bare list_sessions frame and rejects a malformed limit or before with INVALID_MESSAGE without calling listFor** — Verifies that `{type: 'list_sessions'}` is accepted while non-integer or negative `limit` values and malformed `before` cursors (`sessionId` outside 1–128 chars or unparseable `updatedAt`) are rejected with `INVALID_MESSAGE` without invoking `store.listFor`.\n  `src/transport/conversationList.acceptance.test.ts`\n\n## Facets\n\n- [ ] Add `ListSessionsMessage`, `SessionSummaryFrame`, and `SessionListMessage` to `src/types/messages.ts`, include them in `IncomingMessage` and `OutgoingMessage`, and re-export them from `src/index.ts`.\n- [x] Validate a bare `list_sessions` frame in `src/transport/ws/validation.ts` (returns `{type: 'list_sessions'}`; unknown fields such as `userId` are dropped). Round 2026-11-02: `ListSessionsMessage` added to `messages.ts` and `IncomingMessage`.\n- [x] Validate `limit` and `before` on `list_sessions` in `src/transport/ws/validation.ts` (round 2026-11-02: unit tests in `validation.test.ts`; shared `eventually`/`delegatingTo` moved to `wsTestSupport.ts` for lint) (optional non-negative integer `limit`; optional `before` object with 1–128 char `sessionId` and finite-date `updatedAt` string or number; anything malformed is `INVALID_MESSAGE`).\n- [ ] Thread `sessionStore` into `createMessageHandler` (optional parameter so existing 1-arg and 2-arg callers keep working) and wire it in `src/transport/createServer.ts`.\n- [ ] Handle `list_sessions` in `src/transport/ws/messageHandler.ts` (and helper in `src/transport/ws/sender.ts` if needed) by reading `session.userId`, calling `await sessionStore?.listFor?.(session.userId, {limit, before})`, serialising `Date` fields to ISO strings, and on error logging via `log.error('ws', ...)` and sending `ErrorCodes.SERVER_ERROR`.\n- [ ] Document `list_sessions` and `session_list` in `specs/hal-engine-websocket-protocol/spec.md` and `specs/hal-engine-conversation-list/spec.md`.\n\n## Out of scope\n\n- Conversation titles or previews (first user message, model-generated title).\n- Deleting or renaming a conversation from the client over the socket.\n- Switching the socket's active session Deliver a user's conversation list over the WebSocket protocol\n\nIssue: re-cinq/HALEngine#144\nLabels: type:feature, size:M, area:api, area:compliance, epic:extension-points\n\nPart of #35\n\n## Why\n\n#143 gives a consumer `store.listFor(userId)`.\nIt does not give a **client** anything: the browser holding the socket cannot reach the store, so\nevery consumer that wants a history sidebar has to stand up its own authenticated HTTP route over\nthe same data the engine already authenticated on the upgrade. That is a second authorisation\npath to the same personal data, written once per consumer, each one able to get the ownership\ncheck wrong in its own way — the exact failure #45 went out of its way to avoid for resume.\n\nA consumer may have no such route to extend: an engine deployment behind an api with a session\nstore and no conversation endpoints of its own has nothing to add this to. The engine already knows who is connected and already\nreplays a conversation on resume; answering \"which conversations do I have\" over the same socket\nis the smaller surface.\n\n## What to do\n\n- `src/types/messages.ts`, additive only:\n  - `ListConversationsMessage {type: 'list_conversations'; limit?: number; before?: string}` into\n    `IncomingMessage`. There is no user id field, and that is the point: the list is always the\n    connection's authenticated user's.\n  - `ConversationListMessage {type: 'conversation_list'; conversations: ConversationSummary[]}`\n    into `OutgoingMessage`, where `ConversationSummary` is `SessionSummary` with its two times as\n    ISO strings — `Date` does not survive `JSON.stringify` as a `Date`, and the protocol types\n    describe what goes on the wire.\n- `validation.ts`: a `list_conversations` case — `limit`, when present, an integer 1–200;\n  `before`, when present, a string `Date.parse` accepts. Anything else is `INVALID_MESSAGE`, as\n  the two existing cases do it.\n- Handling: `createMessageHandler` takes the session store and the new options alongside the\n  orchestrator (`createServer` already holds both), and answers `list_conversations` on the\n  non-locked path beside `ping` — a history request is not a turn and must not queue behind a\n  streaming answer.\n- The user id comes from `ws.userId`, never from the frame. Add the regression:\n  a frame carrying an extra `userId` of another user is answered with the connecting user's own\n  list.\n- Opt-in: `HalEngineConfig.transport.history?: {enabled: boolean}`, default off, threaded through\n  `createServer`'s options like `resume`. With it off, `list_conversations` is answered with an\n  `error` frame and no list.\n- A store that implements no `listFor` is answered with an `error`, not an empty list: a client\n  that renders \"no conversations\" for a user who has several is a worse answer than one that\n  renders a failure. `ErrorCodes` gains one code for both cases (`UNSUPPORTED`), additive.\n- Picking a conversation needs nothing new: the client reconnects with `?sessionId=<id>`, which\n  #45 already ships with the ownership check and the replay. No in-band switch frame, no second\n  replay path.\n- Specs and docs: extend `specs/hal-engine-websocket-protocol/spec.md` (the two frames, their\n  field tables, the authorisation rule) and the conversation-list spec; add § Conversation list\n  to `docs/getting-started.md` by editing `example/full-config.ts` and running `npm run docs:fix`\n  — that listing sits under `<!-- doc-block: example/full-config.ts#full-config -->` and\n  `npm run docs:check` fails a hand-edited block.\n\n## Decisions\n\n- **A frame, not a REST route.** The REST chat routes are demo-only and are not where a consumer is meant to build. The WebSocket is where the caller is\n  already authenticated, once per connection, by the consumer's own `wsAuth`.\n- **Its own opt-in flag, separate from `resume`.** Resume hands a client back a conversation it\n  was already in; a list tells it what else the user has. A deployer who turned on resume did not\n  thereby agree to the second disclosure, and a config that conflates them cannot be audited.\n- **Answered off the per-session lock.** `PerSessionLock` serialises turns (#47). A list request\n  that queued behind a two-minute streaming answer would look broken, and it mutates nothing.\n- **An error, not an empty list, when it cannot be answered.** See above; silence that looks like\n  data is the one failure mode a history view cannot recover from.\n- **Answered when asked, never pushed.** No `conversation_list` arrives unsolicited: a client\n  that just started a conversation knows, and a push would need a fan-out across the sockets a\n  resumed session can be open on.\n- **The frame is accepted by `validateMessage` whether or not the feature is on**, and the\n  handler is what refuses it. Validation is static and has no config; the alternative is\n  threading options into a pure validator to turn a type into \"unknown\".\n\n## Acceptance criteria\n\n- [ ] `src/types/messages.ts` gains only new interfaces and their two union members (**amended:** four interfaces, not two — each frame needs a type for its own payload, `ConversationCursor` for what a client sends back and `ConversationSummary` for a row on the wire) — a\n      `git diff` of that file touches no existing line, and a fixture pins that every message\n      literal valid before this change still compiles\n- [ ] `validateMessage({type: 'list_conversations'})` is valid; with `limit: 0`, `limit: 201`,\n      `limit: 1.5`, `limit: '10'` or `before: 'not a date'` it is invalid with a message naming\n      the field\n- [ ] With `history: {enabled: true}` and a store holding three conversations for the user, a\n      `list_conversations` frame is answered with exactly one `conversation_list` carrying three\n      summaries, newest first, each `createdAt` and `updatedAt` an ISO string, and no\n      `stream_end`\n- [ ] A frame carrying `{type: 'list_conversations', userId: '<another user>'}` is answered with\n      the connecting user's own list, and the store's `listFor` is called with `ws.userId` —\n      asserted on the recorded argument, not only on the frame\n- [ ] `limit` and `before` reach `listFor` as the frame sent them. **Amended:** `before` is a\n      `{updatedAt, sessionId}` cursor of ISO strings, not a `Date`. This was written before\n      #143's review found that paging on a timestamp alone drops a conversation whenever two\n      share an activity time, so the store's cursor became a composite that accepts an ISO\n      string; a `Date` cannot carry the tie-break. The handler passes `{limit, before}` with\n      both possibly `undefined` rather than omitting the argument, which is the same call\n- [ ] With `history` absent, the same frame is answered with one `error` frame whose code is\n      `UNSUPPORTED` and no `conversation_list`\n- [ ] With `history: {enabled: true}` against a store implementing no `listFor`, the answer is\n      the same `error` frame, and `grep` shows no code path that sends `conversations: []`\n- [ ] A `listFor` that rejects is answered with one `error` frame whose code is `SERVER_ERROR`\n      and whose message carries no store detail, the socket stays open, and the failure is\n      logged with the session id and no user content. **Added during the build:** a store throwing\n      the new exported `StoreCannotList` is answered `UNSUPPORTED` instead, so an injected\n      collection without a `find` reads as the misconfiguration it is rather than a database\n      that failed\n- [ ] A `list_conversations` sent while a `user_message` is streaming on the same session is\n      answered before that turn's `stream_end` — it does not wait on the lock\n- [ ] A client that takes an id from a `conversation_list` and reconnects with `?sessionId=<id>`\n      receives `resumed: true` and that conversation's replay, with no new transport code: the\n      test exercises the existing #45 path end to end\n- [ ] `ConnectedMessage` is unchanged by this issue\n- [ ] **GDPR.** The protocol spec states that `conversation_list` carries one authenticated\n      user's own conversation metadata and no conversation content, that the user id is taken\n      from the connection and never from the frame, and that `transport.history` is the\n      deployer's decision to make a user's history reachable from the client; `SessionResumeOptions`'\n      retention note is mirrored on the new options type, pointing at #41\n- [ ] `CHANGELOG.md` carries an entry under `## [Unreleased]` written for somebody installing the\n      package\n- [ ] The `docs/getting-started.md` change is made in `example/full-config.ts` and the doc\n      regenerated with `npm run docs:fix`, not typed into the markdown\n- [ ] Spec statements carry trailing `([validated by: ...](...#Lnn))` citations, `Status` rows\n      match coverage, and `npm run check:spec-links`, `npm run check:spec-status` and\n      `npm run spec:names` pass\n- [ ] The version bump is MINOR — new optional config, two additive protocol frames, one new\n      error code — and the PR body says so; no migration guide is owed\n- [ ] `npm run verify` passes and the coverage floors for the touched groups hold or rise\n\n## Amendments\n\nThree things changed between writing this issue and building it, each marked in the criterion it\naffects:\n\n1. **`before` is a cursor, not a timestamp.** #143's review found that paging on `updatedAt` alone\n   silently drops a conversation when two share an activity time. The store's cursor became\n   `{updatedAt, sessionId}`, so the frame carries both halves and the validator refuses one half\n   alone. Built as originally written, the transport would have reintroduced the defect the store\n   had just been fixed for.\n2. **`messages.ts` gains four interfaces, not two** — the two frames plus the two payload types\n   they describe.\n3. **`StoreCannotList` is new public surface**, beyond what this issue specified: without it a\n   permanent store misconfiguration cannot be told from a transient failure, in either the client\n   frame or the log.\n\nTwo decisions the criteria did not anticipate:\n\n- **`transport.history` without `transport.resume` is useless, and now says so.** `?sessionId=` is\n  read only when resume is enabled, so a list whose rows cannot be opened is a deployment slip:\n  the engine warns at startup and the documentation says so where it tells a client to reconnect\n  with a row's id. Refusing to start was considered and rejected as too blunt for a library.\n- **The conversation the connection is in is listed like any other**, so a fresh connect sees a row\n  of its own holding no entries. Filtering it out was rejected: it is a real conversation the user\n  is in, and it is their newest the moment they send anything.\n\n## Tech notes\n\nDepends on #143: without `listFor` there is\nnothing to send.\n\nKey paths: `src/types/messages.ts`, `src/types/session.ts` (`ErrorCodes`),\n`src/transport/ws/{validation,messageHandler,sender}.ts`, `src/transport/createServer.ts`,\n`src/config.ts`, `example/full-config.ts`, `specs/hal-engine-websocket-protocol/spec.md`.\n\nOut of scope: a preview or title on the summaries (its own issue); deleting a conversation from\nthe client, which would put an erasure primitive on the wire and needs its own authorisation\ndecision; paging state held server-side — the client pages with `before`; an operator view of\nanother user's conversations, which is an authorisation question of its own; and live sync between open tabs,\nwhich #124 ruled out.\n\nRefs #143, #145, #41, #45, #47, #124\n\n\n\n\n---\n## CI reported failures on 6c0a42c9960a0247e33b28157d3f80cae03e89ab\n\nThe build for your last push is red. sessions}` with `createdAt` and `updatedAt` serialised as ISO-8601 strings.\n  `src/transport/conversationList.acceptance.test.ts`\n\n- [ ] **listing conversations over the WebSocket protocol › sessions: []}` and a store whose `listFor` throws/rejects logs an error under category `'ws'` with the userId and error message and answers `{type: 'error', code: 'SERVER_ERROR'}`.\n {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_018DFHADamqmwhujFvNLF5Py","type":"tool_result","content":"# Lore test-command interface (lore_list_tests / lore_run_test).\n# Written for this repo's Jest suite rather than adapted from the Vitest\n# equivalent for a Vitest suite: the report shapes happen to match, but the\n# invocations do not.\n- id: hal-engine-jest\n  name: HAL Engine Jest suite\n  cwd: .\n  description: >-\n    The ts-jest unit suite over src/ and scripts/ (jest.config.js roots), run\n    on every pull request in .github/workflows/ci.yml as the \"Test\" step.\n    Selectors are \"<repo-relative-file>::<full test name>\" as emitted by the\n    list command. Jest has no dry-run, so the list command executes the suite\n    once with --json and derives every name from the report; it deliberately\n    tolerates a red suite, since listing must not depend on the tests passing.\n    The run command selects one test with --runTestsByPath plus an anchored,\n    regex-escaped -t, and treats a selector that matches nothing as a failure\n    rather than a green run of an empty suite. Neither command builds first:\n    no test here reads dist/.\n  list: node scripts/lore-list-tests.mjs\n  run: node scripts/lore-run-test.mjs \"{selector}\"\n  coverage_format: lcov\n---\nAGENTS.md\nCHANGELOG.md\nCLAUDE.md\nCODE_OF_CONDUCT.md\nCONTRIBUTING.md\nLICENSE\nREADME.md\nSECURITY.md\nadrs\ndocs\neslint.config.backlog.mjs\neslint.config.mjs\nexample\njest.config.js\nlayers.yaml\nnode_modules\npackage-lock.json\npackage.json\nscripts\nsmoke\nspecs\nsrc\ntsconfig.build.json\ntsconfig.example.json\ntsconfig.json\n---\n{\n  \"name\": \"@re-cinq/hal-engine\",\n  \"version\": \"0.5.0\",\n  \"type\": \"module\",\n  \"description\": \"A generic AI chat server framework with pluggable providers, tools, and transport\",\n      \"types\": \"./dist/index.d.ts\",\n      \"default\": \"./dist/index.js\"\n    }\n  },\n  \"files\": [\n    \"dist\"\n  ],\n  \"engines\": {\n    \"node\": \">=22\"\n  },\n  \"publishConfig\": {\n    \"access\": \"public\"\n  },\n  \"config\": {\n    \"mongodbMemoryServer\": {\n      \"version\": \"7.0.14\",\n      \"downloadDir\": \"./node_modules/.cache/mongodb-binaries\"\n    }\n  },\n  \"scripts\": {\n    \"dev\": \"tsx watch example/server.ts\",\n    \"build\": \"node --input-type=module -e \\\"import {rmSync} from 'node:fs'; rmSync('dist',{recursive:true,force:true})\\\" && tsc -p tsconfig.build.json\",\n    \"prepare\": \"npm run build\",\n    \"start\": \"node dist/index.js\",\n    \"test\": \"node --experimental-vm-modules node_modules/jest/bin/jest.js\",\n    \"test:coverage\": \"node --experimental-vm-modules node_modules/jest/bin/jest.js --coverage\",\n    \"smoke\": \"bash scripts/smoke.sh\",\n    \"typecheck\": \"tsc --noEmit\",\n {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01SuMo5qiKPVLQW7yx4Viy4V","type":"tool_result","content":"<persisted-output>\nOutput too large (34.9KB). Full output saved to: /var/tmp/loop-bench/runs/5c5b1f933f9e/home-3/.claude/projects/-var-tmp-loop-bench-runs-5c5b1f933f9e-workspace/d8bb752a-353c-43ff-8165-f0337f4989a3/tool-results/b6s0jchse.txt\n\nPreview (first 2KB):\n# Changelog\n\nAll notable changes to `@re-cinq/hal-engine` are documented here.\n\nThe format is [Keep a Changelog 1.1.0](https://keepachangelog.com/en/1.1.0/), and this project adheres to\n[Semantic Versioning](https://semver.org/spec/v2.0.0.html). Entries are written for somebody installing the\npackage, not for somebody reading this repository's commit log.\n\n## [Unreleased]\n\n### Added\n\n- `SessionStore.listFor(userId, options?)`, an optional store member that answers the summaries of a user's conversations — `sessionId`, `createdAt`, `updatedAt` and `entryCount` — most recent activity first, so a consumer can build a conversation list instead of only rejoining one. Nothing in the engine calls it yet: the store answers the list, and the route or frame that carries it to a client is still yours to write. `InMemorySessionStore` and `MongoSessionStore` both implement it; a store of your own may leave it out. A summary never carries a conversation's entries or its credentials. Page with `limit` (50 by default, 200 at most, and `0` answers nothing) and `before`, which takes the last summary you saw: the cursor carries both `updatedAt` and `sessionId`, so two conversations saved in the same millisecond cannot straddle a page boundary and go unlisted. See `docs/session-stores.md` § Listing a user's conversations.\n- The WebSocket protocol accepts a `list_sessions` frame, `{type: 'list_sessions', limit?, before?}`, with `limit` a non-negative integer and `before` a `{updatedAt, sessionId}` cursor; a malformed one is answered `INVALID_MESSAGE`. The answer, a `session_list` frame for the connecting user, is not wired up yet.\n\n### Changed\n\n- `MongoSessionStore` now writes an `entryCount` field beside the entries on every save, so a conversation list can count a conversation without reading one. A document saved by an earlier version lists as a conversation of no entries until you backfill it; `docs/session-stores.md` carries the one-line command.\n...\n</persisted-output>","is_error":false}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"d8bb752a-353c-43ff-8165-f0337f4989a3","uuid":"e0482f5a-5e0f-4c44-b435-24d381de1a3b","timestamp":"2026-11-02T11:24:27.385Z","tool_use_result":{"stdout":"# Changelog\n\nAll notable changes to `@re-cinq/hal-engine` are documented here.\n\nThe format is [Keep a Changelog 1.1.0](https://keepachangelog.com/en/1.1.0/), and this project adheres to\n[Semantic Versioning](https://semver.org/spec/v2.0.0.html). Entries are written for somebody installing the\npackage, not for somebody reading this repository's commit log.\n\n## [Unreleased]\n\n### Added\n\n- `SessionStore.listFor(userId, options?)`, an optional store member that answers the summaries of a user's conversations — `sessionId`, `createdAt`, `updatedAt` and `entryCount` — most recent activity first, so a consumer can build a conversation list instead of only rejoining one. Nothing in the engine calls it yet: the store answers the list, and the route or frame that carries it to a client is still yours to write. `InMemorySessionStore` and `MongoSessionStore` both implement it; a store of your own may leave it out. A summary never carries a conversation's entries or its credentials. Page with `limit` (50 by default, 200 at most, and `0` answers nothing) and `before`, which takes the last summary you saw: the cursor carries both `updatedAt` and `sessionId`, so two conversations saved in the same millisecond cannot straddle a page boundary and go unlisted. See `docs/session-stores.md` § Listing a user's conversations.\n- The WebSocket protocol accepts a `list_sessions` frame, `{type: 'list_sessions', limit?, before?}`, with `limit` a non-negative integer and `before` a `{updatedAt, sessionId}` cursor; a malformed one is answered `INVALID_MESSAGE`. The answer, a `session_list` frame for the connecting user, is not wired up yet.\n\n### Changed\n\n- `MongoSessionStore` now writes an `entryCount` field beside the entries on every save, so a conversation list can count a conversation without reading one. A document saved by an earlier version lists as a conversation of no entries until you backfill it; `docs/session-stores.md` carries the one-line command.\n- `MongoSessionStore.latestFor` now breaks a tie on `updatedAt` by `_id`, so it and `listFor` agree on which conversation is newest when two were saved in the same millisecond. Which of two tied conversations a resume rejoins can therefore change.\n- The index to create for `MongoSessionStore` is now `{userId: 1, updatedAt: -1, _id: -1}`. The two-key form that served `latestFor` cannot satisfy `listFor`'s sort, so Mongo would sort a user's whole history on every page. `docs/session-stores.md` § Latest session has the command.\n---\ndiff --git a/CHANGELOG.md b/CHANGELOG.md\nindex b36c826..48e0596 100644\n--- a/CHANGELOG.md\n+++ b/CHANGELOG.md\n@@ -11,6 +11,7 @@ package, not for somebody reading this repository's commit log.\n ### Added\n \n - `SessionStore.listFor(userId, options?)`, an optional store member that answers the summaries of a user's conversations — `sessionId`, `createdAt`, `updatedAt` and `entryCount` — most recent activity first, so a consumer can build a conversation list instead of only rejoining one. Nothing in the engine calls it yet: the store answers the list, and the route or frame that carries it to a client is still yours to write. `InMemorySessionStore` and `MongoSessionStore` both implement it; a store of your own may leave it out. A summary never carries a conversation's entries or its credentials. Page with `limit` (50 by default, 200 at most, and `0` answers nothing) and `before`, which takes the last summary you saw: the cursor carries both `updatedAt` and `sessionId`, so two conversations saved in the same millisecond cannot straddle a page boundary and go unlisted. See `docs/session-stores.md` § Listing a user's conversations.\n+- The WebSocket protocol accepts a `list_sessions` frame, `{type: 'list_sessions', limit?, before?}`, with `limit` a non-negative integer and `before` a `{updatedAt, sessionId}` cursor; a malformed one is answered `INVALID_MESSAGE`. The answer, a `session_list` frame for the connecting user, is not wired up yet.\n \n ### Changed\n \ndiff --git a/specs/hal-engine-conversation-list/spec.md a/specs/hal-engine-conversation-list/spec.md b/specs/hal-engine-conversation-list/spec.md\nindex c9ee424..e9fbd44 100644\n--- a/specs/hal-engine-conversation-list/spec.md\n+++ b/specs/hal-engine-conversation-list/spec.md\n@@ -68,3 +68,9 @@ Resume gets a client back into one conversation: the id it names, or the user's\n ## When the store cannot answer\n \n - A store whose collection fails rejects rather than answering a partial or empty list: what to show a user who may have conversations is the caller's decision, and an empty history is not a safe guess ([validated by: rejects rather than answering a partial list when the collection fails](../../src/infrastructure/stores/mongo/mongoConversationList.test.ts#L225)).\n+\n+## Over the WebSocket protocol\n+\n+- A client sends `{type: 'list_sessions', limit?, before?}` and receives `{type: 'session_list', sessions}` for the socket's authenticated user, with `createdAt` and `updatedAt` serialised as ISO-8601 strings and any client-supplied `userId` ignored ([validated by: answers list_sessions for the authenticated user with ISO dates, forwarding limit and before while ignoring any client userId](../../src/transport/conversationList.acceptance.test.ts#L74)).\n+- A store without `listFor` answers `{type: 'session_list', sessions: []}`, and a throwing `listFor` logs an error on the `ws` category and answers `{type: 'error', code: 'SERVER_ERROR'}` ([validated by: answers an empty session_list when the store has no listFor, and SERVER_ERROR with a ws error log when listFor throws](../../src/transport/conversationList.acceptance.test.ts#L115)).\n+- The validator accepts an optional non-negative integer `limit` and an optional `before` cursor whose `sessionId` is 1–128 characters and whose `updatedAt` parses as a valid timestamp, rejecting any other `list_sessions` payload with `INVALID_MESSAGE` without calling `listFor` ([validated by: accepts a bare list_sessions frame and rejects a malformed limit or before with INVALID_MESSAGE without calling listFor](../../src/transport/conversationList.acceptance.test.ts#L144)).\ndiff --git a/specs/hal-engine-session-resume/spec.md b/specs/hal-engine-session-resume/spec.md\nindex 839155f..96414c8 100644\n--- a/specs/hal-engine-session-resume/spec.md\n+++ b/specs/hal-engine-session-resume/spec.md\n@@ -16,42 +16,42 @@ A reconnect used to resume nothing: the server minted a fresh id on every connec\n \n ## Opt-in\n \n-- Resume is the optional `transport.resume` config, off by default; without it, a reconnect naming an earlier id gets a new id, a `connected` frame with exactly its four original fields, and no replay ([validated by: with resume absent, answers a reconnect naming the first id with a new id and no replay](../../src/transport/sessionResume.test.ts#L180)).\n-- With resume off, a socket opened at `{basePath}/ws/undefined` still completes the handshake and gets a server-minted id ([validated by: with resume off, still completes a handshake at /hal/ws/undefined and mints an id](../../src/transport/sessionResume.test.ts#L412)).\n+- Resume is the optional `transport.resume` config, off by default; without it, a reconnect naming an earlier id gets a new id, a `connected` frame with exactly its four original fields, and no replay ([validated by: with resume absent, answers a reconnect naming the first id with a new id and no replay](../../src/transport/sessionResume.test.ts#L167)).\n+- With resume off, a socket opened at `{basePath}/ws/undefined` still completes the handshake and gets a server-minted id ([validated by: with resume off, still completes a handshake at /hal/ws/undefined and mints an id](../../src/transport/sessionResume.test.ts#L399)).\n \n ## Rejoining a conversation\n \n-- The owner reconnecting with the id from their first `connected` frame receives `resumed: true` and `entryCount` equal to the stored entries, followed by one `entry_upsert` per entry at its own index, equal to what is stored ([validated by: with resume on, answers the owner reconnecting with resumed true, entryCount 2 and both stored entries](../../src/transport/sessionResume.test.ts#L196)).\n-- A resumed session takes the credentials of the socket that resumed it, and the socket it replaced closing afterwards leaves them alone, though that close still calls `onDisconnect`, since the hooks are per connection ([validated by: keeps the credentials of the socket that resumed a session when the socket it replaced closes](../../src/transport/sessionResume.test.ts#L262)).\n-- A session open on more than one socket carries the credentials of the newest; when that socket closes, it takes those of the newest one still open, so a tab left open keeps working, and they are cleared only when the last one closes ([validated by: gives a session back the credentials of a socket still open when the socket that resumed it closes](../../src/transport/sessionResume.test.ts#L281)).\n-- `onConnect` receives `{resumed}` as its second argument: `false` for the connection that started a session and `true` for each one that resumed it ([validated by: tells onConnect whether a connection started its session or resumed it](../../src/transport/sessionResume.test.ts#L301)).\n-- An entry still streaming when its session is resumed, because its turn is running on the socket that started it, is replayed with `isStreaming: false` and `truncated: true`, since its deltas and commit go to that socket and a client shown it streaming would wait for a commit that never comes, while the stored entry stays as the running turn leaves it ([validated by: replays an entry whose turn is still running as finished and truncated, leaving the stored entry as it is](../../src/transport/sessionResume.test.ts#L364)).\n-- A stored entry a tool suppressed is replayed as `entry_skip`, so the client sees the conversation it saw live and never the text a tool hid ([validated by: replays a suppressed entry as a skip, so the client never sees text a tool suppressed](../../src/transport/sessionResume.test.ts#L328)).\n+- The owner reconnecting with the id from their first `connected` frame receives `resumed: true` and `entryCount` equal to the stored entries, followed by one `entry_upsert` per entry at its own index, equal to what is stored ([validated by: with resume on, answers the owner reconnecting with resumed true, entryCount 2 and both stored entries](../../src/transport/sessionResume.test.ts#L183)).\n+- A resumed session takes the credentials of the socket that resumed it, and the socket it replaced closing afterwards leaves them alone, though that close still calls `onDisconnect`, since the hooks are per connection ([validated by: keeps the credentials of the socket that resumed a session when the socket it replaced closes](../../src/transport/sessionResume.test.ts#L249)).\n+- A session open on more than one socket carries the credentials of the newest; when that socket closes, it takes those of the newest one still open, so a tab left open keeps working, and they are cleared only when the last one closes ([validated by: gives a session back the credentials of a socket still open when the socket that resumed it closes](../../src/transport/sessionResume.test.ts#L268)).\n+- `onConnect` receives `{resumed}` as its second argument: `false` for the connection that started a session and `true` for each one that resumed it ([validated by: tells onConnect whether a connection started its session or resumed it](../../src/transport/sessionResume.test.ts#L288)).\n+- An entry still streaming when its session is resumed, because its turn is running on the socket that started it, is replayed with `isStreaming: false` and `truncated: true`, since its deltas and commit go to that socket and a client shown it streaming would wait for a commit that never comes, while the stored entry stays as the running turn leaves it ([validated by: replays an entry whose turn is still running as finished and truncated, leaving the stored entry as it is](../../src/transport/sessionResume.test.ts#L351)).\n+- A stored entry a tool suppressed is replayed as `entry_skip`, so the client sees the conversation it saw live and never the text a tool hid ([validated by: replays a suppressed entry as a skip, so the client never sees text a tool suppressed](../../src/transport/sessionResume.test.ts#L315)).\n - For that, suppression is recorded on the stored entry: an assistant entry the client was sent and then shown blank is marked `suppressed`, and keeps its text for the model's history ([validated by: marks a sent assistant entry it blanked as suppressed in the session, keeping its text](../../src/transport/ws/messageHandler.test.ts#L237)).\n - A segment opened after suppression, which the client was only ever sent as a skip, is marked `suppressed` too ([validated by: marks a segment opened after suppression as suppressed in the session](../../src/transport/ws/messageHandler.test.ts#L246)).\n-- An answer still open when suppression begins is first committed as streamed so far, then retracted and marked like any sent answer, and the text after it opens a new entry the client is only sent as a skip, so a replay leaves the client as blank as the live stream did ([validated by: commits and retracts an answer still open when suppression begins, and skips what follows as a new entry](../../src/transport/ws/messageHandler.test.ts#L267), [validated by: replays an answer suppressed while it was still open as skips, as blank as the live client was left](../../src/transport/sessionResume.test.ts#L351)).\n+- An answer still open when suppression begins is first committed as streamed so far, then retracted and marked like any sent answer, and the text after it opens a new entry the client is only sent as a skip, so a replay leaves the client as blank as the live stream did ([validated by: commits and retracts an answer still open when suppression begins, and skips what follows as a new entry](../../src/transport/ws/messageHandler.test.ts#L267), [validated by: replays an answer suppressed while it was still open as skips, as blank as the live client was left](../../src/transport/sessionResume.test.ts#L338)).\n - A thinking entry committed before suppression began is left unmarked, since the client kept seeing it, matching what the live stream showed ([validated by: leaves a thinking entry the client kept seeing unmarked, and marks the blanked answer](../../src/transport/ws/messageHandler.test.ts#L255)).\n - A thought still open when suppression begins is committed as the client saw it and left unmarked, and its remaining text opens a new entry marked `suppressed` ([validated by: commits a thought still open when suppression begins as the client saw it, and skips what follows](../../src/transport/ws/messageHandler.test.ts#L288)).\n \n ## Ownership and existence\n \n-- An id is resumed only for the stored session's own user: another user naming it receives a fresh session, `resumed: false` and `resumeFailure: 'unknown'`, with frames identical to the response for an id that was never issued, so the answer cannot be used to learn which ids exist ([validated by: answers another user naming the id exactly as it answers an id that was never issued](../../src/transport/sessionResume.test.ts#L213)).\n-- An id that was never issued receives `resumed: false` with `resumeFailure: 'unknown'`, no replay and no `error` frame ([validated by: answers a never-issued id with resumed false, resumeFailure unknown, and no replay or error](../../src/transport/sessionResume.test.ts#L227)).\n-- A requested id is never adopted as the key of a new session, which would be session fixation: the store then holds one session, under the id the server minted ([validated by: never adopts a requested id as a key: one session, under the id the server minted](../../src/transport/sessionResume.test.ts#L240)).\n+- An id is resumed only for the stored session's own user: another user naming it receives a fresh session, `resumed: false` and `resumeFailure: 'unknown'`, with frames identical to the response for an id that was never issued, so the answer cannot be used to learn which ids exist ([validated by: answers another user naming the id exactly as it answers an id that was never issued](../../src/transport/sessionResume.test.ts#L200)).\n+- An id that was never issued receives `resumed: false` with `resumeFailure: 'unknown'`, no replay and no `error` frame ([validated by: answers a never-issued id with resumed false, resumeFailure unknown, and no replay or error](../../src/transport/sessionResume.test.ts#L214)).\n+- A requested id is never adopted as the key of a new session, which would be session fixation: the store then holds one session, under the id the server minted ([validated by: never adopts a requested id as a key: one session, under the id the server minted](../../src/transport/sessionResume.test.ts#L227)).\n \n ## The store decides what is live\n \n-- Whether an id is resumable is the store's `get` answer: an id it returns a session for is resumed, and one it returns nothing for starts fresh ([validated by: resumes the id its store knows and starts fresh for one it does not](../../src/transport/sessionResume.test.ts#L313)).\n-- A resumed session whose socket closes before its replay is kept, never evicted: eviction erases, and it is reserved for a new session no client ever received ([validated by: keeps a resumed session whose socket closed before the replay, rather than evicting it](../../src/transport/sessionResume.test.ts#L378)).\n-- A socket closing under resume still calls `onDisconnect` with its session id ([validated by: still calls onDisconnect with the session id when a socket closes under resume](../../src/transport/sessionResume.test.ts#L253)).\n+- Whether an id is resumable is the store's `get` answer: an id it returns a session for is resumed, and one it returns nothing for starts fresh ([validated by: resumes the id its store knows and starts fresh for one it does not](../../src/transport/sessionResume.test.ts#L300)).\n+- A resumed session whose socket closes before its replay is kept, never evicted: eviction erases, and it is reserved for a new session no client ever received ([validated by: keeps a resumed session whose socket closed before the replay, rather than evicting it](../../src/transport/sessionResume.test.ts#L365)).\n+- A socket closing under resume still calls `onDisconnect` with its session id ([validated by: still calls onDisconnect with the session id when a socket closes under resume](../../src/transport/sessionResume.test.ts#L240)).\n \n ## Continuing the latest session\n \n-- With `latest` on, a connect that names no `?sessionId=` rejoins the user's most recently active session, which the store's optional `latestFor(userId)` returns, with the same `resumed: true`, `entryCount`, replay and `onConnect` `{resumed: true}` as a named resume ([validated by: with latest on, rejoins the most recently active session of a user whose connect names none](../../src/transport/sessionResume.test.ts#L428)).\n-- It starts a new session for a user who has none, and for a connect that sends `?new=1`, which is how a client starts a new conversation on purpose while a latest one exists ([validated by: with latest on, starts a new session for a user with none, and on ?new=1 even when one exists](../../src/transport/sessionResume.test.ts#L445)).\n-- A connect that names an id rejoins that session, whichever is latest ([validated by: with latest on, still rejoins the session a connect names rather than the latest one](../../src/transport/sessionResume.test.ts#L460)).\n-- A session `latestFor` answers for another user is never rejoined, and a `latestFor` that throws or that the store does not implement starts a new session ([validated by: starts a new session when the store's latestFor answers another user's session, throws, or is absent](../../src/transport/sessionResume.test.ts#L472)).\n-- Without `latest`, a connect that names no id starts a new session, as before ([validated by: with resume on but latest off, starts a new session for a connect that names none](../../src/transport/sessionResume.test.ts#L496)).\n+- With `latest` on, a connect that names no `?sessionId=` rejoins the user's most recently active session, which the store's optional `latestFor(userId)` returns, with the same `resumed: true`, `entryCount`, replay and `onConnect` `{resumed: true}` as a named resume ([validated by: with latest on, rejoins the most recently active session of a user whose connect names none](../../src/transport/sessionResume.test.ts#L415)).\n+- It starts a new session for a user who has none, and for a connect that sends `?new=1`, which is how a client starts a new conversation on purpose while a latest one exists ([validated by: with latest on, starts a new session for a user with none, and on ?new=1 even when one exists](../../src/transport/sessionResume.test.ts#L432)).\n+- A connect that names an id rejoins that session, whichever is latest ([validated by: with latest on, still rejoins the session a connect reads exactly ?new=1 inside the base path as a request for a new session, and nothing else](../../src/transport/ws/helpers.test.ts#L40)).\n - `InMemorySessionStore.latestFor` goes by a session's newest timed entry, so the conversation the user last wrote to beats one created later, and an entry with no time, such as a thought, counts for nothing ([validated by: returns the user's session with {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01N2Yq1JEVgTdDwfNezL5r7L","type":"tool_result","content":"     1\timport type {AddressInfo} from 'node:net';\n     2\timport {WebSocket} from 'ws';\n     3\timport type {Express} from 'express';\n     4\timport {createServer} from './createServer.js';\n     5\timport {HAL_WS_SUBPROTOCOL} from './ws/subprotocol.js';\n     6\timport {answeringOrchestrator, delegatingTo, eventually} from './wsTestSupport.js';\n     7\timport {InMemorySessionStore} from '../infrastructure/stores/inMemorySessionStore.js';\n     8\timport type {SessionCursor, SessionListOptions, SessionStore, SessionSummary} from '../types/sessionStore.js';\n     9\timport {setLogger} from '../shared/logger.js';\n    10\t\n    11\t// Real upgrades over a real socket: what the list_sessions frame answers is the contract, and no double can observe it.\n    12\t\n    13\tinterface Frame {\n    14\t  type: string;\n    15\t  sessionId?: string;\n    16\t  sessions?: Array<{sessionId: string; createdAt: string; updatedAt: string; entryCount: number}>;\n    17\t  code?: string;\n    18\t  message?: string;\n    19\t}\n    20\t\n    21\tconst servers: Array<ReturnType<typeof createServer>> = [];\n    22\tconst clients: WebSocket[] = [];\n    23\tconst logLines: Array<{level: string; category: string; message: string; fields?: Record<string, unknown>}> = [];\n    24\t\n    25\tbeforeEach(() => {\n    26\t  logLines.length = 0;\n    27\t  const record = (level: string) => (category: string, message: string, fields?: Record<string, unknown>) =>\n    28\t    void logLines.push({level, category, message, fields});\n    29\t  setLogger({debug: record('debug'), info: record('info'), warn: record('warn'), error: record('error')});\n    30\t});\n    31\t\n    32\tafterEach(async () => {\n    33\t  for (const client of clients.splice(0)) client.terminate();\n    34\t  for (const hal of servers.splice(0)) {\n    35\t    const {wss} = hal;\n    36\t    await eventually(() => wss.clients.size === 0);\n    37\t    await hal.stop();\n    38\t  }\n    39\t  setLogger();\n    40\t});\n    41\t\n    42\tconst startEngine = async (store: SessionStore, userId: string | number = 'u1') => {\n    43\t  const hal = createServer({\n    44\t    app: (() => undefined) as unknown as Express,\n    45\t    wsAuth: async () => ({id: userId}),\n    46\t    sessionStore: store,\n    47\t    orchestrator: answeringOrchestrator,\n    48\t  });\n    49\t  await hal.start(0);\n    50\t  servers.push(hal);\n    51\t  const {port} = hal.server.address() as AddressInfo;\n    52\t  return `ws://127.0.0.1:${port}/hal/ws`;\n    53\t};\n    54\t\n    55\tconst askAfterConnect = async (url: string, outbound: unknown): Promise<{connected: Frame; frames: Frame[]}> => {\n    56\t  const client = new WebSocket(url, [HAL_WS_SUBPROTOCOL, 't']);\n    57\t  clients.push(client);\n    58\t  const frames: Frame[] = [];\n    59\t  let connected: Frame = {type: ''};\n    60\t  await new Promise<void>(resolve => {\n    61\t    client.on('message', raw => {\n    62\t      const frame = JSON.parse(String(raw)) as Frame;\n    63\t      if (frame.type === 'pong') return resolve();\n    64\t      if (frame.type !== 'connected') return void frames.push(frame);\n    65\t      connected = frame;\n    66\t      client.send(typeof outbound === 'string' ? outbound : JSON.stringify(outbound));\n    67\t      client.send(JSON.stringify({type: 'ping', timestamp: 1}));\n    68\t    });\n    69\t  });\n    70\t  return {connected, frames};\n    71\t};\n    72\t\n    73\tdescribe('listing conversations over the WebSocket protocol', () => {\n    74\t  it('answers list_sessions for the authenticated user with ISO dates, forwarding limit and before while ignoring any client userId', async () => {\n    75\t    const inner = new InMemorySessionStore();\n    76\t    const seen: Array<{userId: string | number; options?: SessionListOptions}> = [];\n    77\t    const summaries: SessionSummary[] = [\n    78\t      {\n    79\t        sessionId: 's2',\n    80\t        createdAt: new Date('2026-01-01T00:00:00.000Z'),\n    81\t        updatedAt: new Date('2026-01-01T00:02:00.000Z'),\n    82\t        entryCount: 4,\n    83\t      },\n    84\t    ];\n    85\t    const store: SessionStore = {\n    86\t      ...delegatingTo(inner),\n    87\t      listFor: (userId, options) => {\n    88\t        seen.push({userId, options});\n    89\t        return summaries;\n    90\t Pick<HalServerOptions, 'sessionStore'>\n    39\t): Promise<{url: string; hal: HalServer}> {\n    40\t  const hal = createServer({\n    41\t    app: (() => undefined) as unknown as Express,\n    42\t const {port} = hal.server.address() as AddressInfo;\n    49\t  return {url: `ws://127.0.0.1:${port}/hal/ws/c1`, hal};\n    50\t}\n    51\t\n    52\t/** Opens a client that the suite's teardown will close, whether its test passed or failed. */\n    53\texport function connectClient(url: string, token = 't'): WebSocket {\n    54\t  const client = new WebSocket(url, [HAL_WS_SUBPROTOCOL, token]);\n function deferred(): {until: Promise<void>; release: () => void} {\n    61\t  let release = (): void => undefined;\n    62\t  const until = new Promise<void>(resolve => {\n    63\t    release = function framesUntil(client: WebSocket, until: string): Promise<string[]> {\n    70\t  const seen: string[] = [];\n    71\t  return new Promise<string[]>(resolve => {\n    72\t    client.on('message', raw => {\n    73\t      const {type} = JSON.parse(String(raw)) as {type: string};\n    74\t      seen.push(type);\n    75\t      if (type === until) resolve(seen);\n    76\t    });\n    77\t  });\n    78\t}\n    79\t\n    80\t/** Opens a client that asks one question the moment the socket opens, resolving on the first `until` frame. */\n    81\texport function askOnOpen(\n    82\t  url: string,\n    83\t  {until = 'stream_end', token}: {until?: string; token?: string} = {}\n    84\t): {client: WebSocket; finished: Promise<string[]>} {\n    85\t  const client = connectClient(url, token);\n    86\t  const finished = framesUntil(client, until);\n    87\t  client.on('open', () => client.send(JSON.stringify({type: 'user_message', content: 'hello'})));\n    88\t  return {client, finished};\n    89\t}\n    90\t\n    91\t/** The session id the client's `connected` frame announces, read after the fact; attach it before any frame arrives. */\n    92\texport function announcedId(client: WebSocket): () => string {\n    93\t  let sessionId = '';\n    94\t  client.on('message', raw => {\n    95\t    const frame = JSON.parse(String(raw)) as {type: string; sessionId?: string};\n    96\t '';\n    97\t  });\n    98\t  return () => sessionId;\n    99\t}\n   100\t\n   101\t/** Tears every socket and server down, so a red test reports instead of hanging the run. */\n   102\texport async function stopEngines(): Promise<void> {\n   103\t  for (const client of clients.splice(0)) client.terminate();\n   104\t  for (const hal of servers.splice(0)) await hal.stop();\n   105\t}\n   106\t\n   107\t/** Polls `check` for up to half a second, so teardown waits for a socket to close without hanging. */\n   108\texport const eventually = async (check: () => boolean): Promise<void> => {\n   109\t  for (let attempt = 0; attempt < 50 && !check(); attempt++) await new Promise(resolve => setTimeout(resolve, 10));\n   110\t};\n   111\t\n   112\t/** A store with only the five required members, so an optional one can be added, replaced or left out per test. */\n   113\texport const delegatingTo = (inner: InMemorySessionStore): SessionStore => ({\n   114\t  create: (sessionId, userId, options) => inner.create(sessionId, userId, options),\n   115\t  get: sessionId => inner.get(sessionId),\n   116\t  delete: sessionId => inner.delete(sessionId),\n   117\t  count: () => inner.count(),\n   118\t  clear: () => inner.clear(),\n   119\t});\n   120\t\n   121\t/** The one session a store holds, for asserting what survived a close. */\n   122\texport async function onlySession(store: SessionStore, sessionId: string): Promise<ChatSession | undefined> {\n   123\t  return (await store.get(sessionId)) as ChatSession | undefined;\n 89\t}\n    90\t\n    91\t/** The session id the client's `connected` frame announces, read after the fact; attach it before any frame arrives. */\n    92\texport function announcedId(client: WebSocket): () => string {\n    93\t  let sessionId = '';\n    94\t  client.on('message', raw => {\n    95\t    const frame = JSON.parse(String(raw)) as {type: string; sessionId?: string};\n    96\t    if (frame.type === 'connected') sessionId = frame.sessionId ?? '';\n    97\t  });\n    98\t  return () => sessionId;\n    99\t}\n   100\t\n   101\t/** Tears every socket and server down, so a red test reports instead of hanging the run. */\n   102\texport async function stopEngines(): Promise<void> {\n   103\t  for (const client of clients.splice(0)) client.terminate();\n   104\t  for (const hal of servers.splice(0)) await hal.stop();\n   105\t}\n   106\t\n   107\t/** Polls `check` for up to half a second, so teardown waits for a socket to close without hanging. */\n   108\texport const eventually = async (check: () => boolean): Promise<void> => {\n   109\t  for (let attempt = 0; attempt < 50 && !check(); attempt++) await new Promise(resolve => setTimeout(resolve, 10));\n   110\t};\n   111\t\n   112\t/** A store with only the five required members, so an optional one can be added, replaced or left out per test. */\n   113\texport const delegatingTo = (inner: InMemorySessionStore): SessionStore => ({\n   114\t  create: (sessionId, userId, options) => inner.create(sessionId, userId, options),\n   115\t  get: sessionId => inner.get(sessionId),\n   116\t  delete: sessionId => inner.delete(sessionId),\n   117\t  count: () => inner.count(),\n   118\t  clear: () => inner.clear(),\n   119\t});\n   120\t\n   121\t/** The one session a store holds, for asserting what survived a close. */\n   122\texport async function onlySession(store: SessionStore, sessionId: string): Promise<ChatSession | undefined> {\n   123\t  return (await store.get(sessionId)) as ChatSession | undefined;\n   124\t}","stderr":"","interrupted":false,"isImage":false,"noOutputExpected":false},"tool_result_meta":[{"id":"toolu_01N2Yq1JEVgTdDwfNezL5r7L","permission_decision":{"decision":"accept","source":"config","reason_type":"mode"}}]}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01K1NikXTvfX8JRqmwRp2tar","type":"tool_result","content":"822e42d feat(transport): validate list_sessions limit and before (#144)\nf291811 test(transport): add red acceptance tests for list_sessions over WebSocket (#144)\n---\nFrom /var/tmp/loop-bench/runs/5c5b1f933f9e/origin\n * branch            main       -> FETCH_HEAD\n---\n#!/usr/bin/env bash\n# Fails when a diff touches src/ and leaves CHANGELOG.md alone.\n#\n# Usage:\n#   bash scripts/check-changelog.sh [base-ref]     # default: origin/main\n#\n# The rule is about what a consumer can observe, not about effort: anything that\n# changes shipped behaviour needs a sentence they can read on npm. A change to\n# src/ that genuinely is not user-visible - an internal refactor, a test-only\n# edit - takes the `no-changelog` label instead, which is a deliberate, visible\n# act rather than a silent omission.\nset -uo pipefail\n\nbase=\"${1:-origin/main}\"\n\n# A base ref that does not resolve means nothing was compared. On a laptop that\n# is ordinary - a fresh clone has no origin/main - and skipping is right. In CI\n# it is the gate passing without running, which is the one outcome a gate must\n# never have, and it is exactly how a fetch that failed went unnoticed.\nif ! git rev-parse --verify --quiet \"$base\" >/dev/null; then\n  if [ -n \"${GITHUB_ACTIONS:-}\" ]; then\n    echo \"check-changelog: FAILED - base ref '$base' does not resolve, so nothing was checked\" >&2\n    exit 1\n  fi\n  echo \"check-changelog: base ref '$base' not found; skipping\" >&2\n  exit 0\nfi\n\nmerge_base=\"$(git merge-base \"$base\" HEAD)\"\nchanged=\"$(git diff --name-only \"$merge_base\"..HEAD)\"\n\nsrc_changed=\"$(printf '%s\\n' \"$changed\" | grep -E '^src/' | grep -vE '\\.test\\.ts$' || true)\"\n\nif [ -z \"$src_changed\" ]; then\n  echo \"check-changelog: no shipped source changed since ${base}.\"\n  exit 0\nfi\n\nif printf '%s\\n' \"$changed\" | grep -qx 'CHANGELOG.md'; then\n  echo \"check-changelog: src/ changed and CHANGELOG.md was updated.\"\n  exit 0\nfi\n\n{\n  echo \"check-changelog: these files under src/ changed since ${base} and CHANGELOG.md did not:\"\n  printf '%s\\n' \"$src_changed\" | sed 's/^/  /'\n  echo\n  echo \"Add an entry under ## [Unreleased], written for somebody installing the package.\"\n  echo \"If the change genuinely is not user-visible, apply the 'no-changelog' label to the pull request.\"\n} >&2\nexit 1\n---\ncheck-spec-anchor-names:  794 citation(s) name the test they point at\nexit=0\n---\nmisplaced: 0 across 0 specs (1911 statements scanned)\n---\nspec-status: 0 findings across 0 docs (33 scanned)","is_error":false}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"d8bb752a-353c-43ff-8165-f0337f4989a3","uuid":"ebb80c65-0427-46bb-805f-50f5a66939f5","timestamp":"2026-11-02T11:24:57.755Z","tool_use_result":{"stdout":"822e42d feat(transport): validate list_sessions limit and before (#144)\nf291811 test(transport): add red acceptance tests for list_sessions over WebSocket (#144)\n---\nFrom /var/tmp/loop-bench/runs/5c5b1f933f9e/origin\n * branch            main       -> FETCH_HEAD\n---\n#!/usr/bin/env bash\n# Fails when a diff touches src/ and leaves CHANGELOG.md alone.\n#\n# Usage:\n#   bash scripts/check-changelog.sh [base-ref]     # default: origin/main\n#\n# The rule is about what a consumer can observe, not about effort: anything that\n# changes shipped behaviour needs a sentence they can read on npm.
